(\mathscr{X}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1}),\dotsc,(\mathscr{X}_{m},\mathcal{A}_{m})
, we equip the disjoint union
\bigsqcup_{\,k=1}^{\,m}\mathscr{X}_{k}
with the
\sigma
-algebra
\bigl\{\bigsqcup_{\,k=1}^{\,m}A_{k}:A_{k}\in\mathcal{A}_{k}\text{ for all }k\bigr\}
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
be a sub-
\sigma
-algebra and let
X\colon(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})\to\bigsqcup_{\,k=1}^{\,n}\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
be a measurable function. Suppose that there is a partition of
\Omega
into disjoint events
\Omega_{1},\dotsc,\Omega_{m}\in\mathcal{G}
such that for each
k=1,\dotsc,m
, the map
X
takes values in
\mathbb{R}^{n_{k}\times n_{k}}
on
\Omega_{k}
and has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})
given
\mathcal{G}
on
\Omega_{k}
, for some (deterministic)
n_{k}\in\{1,\dotsc,n\}
. Moreover, let
Q=(Q_{1}\;Q_{2})\colon(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})\to\bigsqcup_{\,k=1}^{\,n}\mathbb{O}_{k}
be a
\mathcal{G}
-measurable function such that on each event
\Omega_{k}
, the map
Q
takes values in
\mathbb{O}_{n_{k}}
, and
Q_{1},Q_{2}
have
\ell_{k}
and
n_{k}-\ell_{k}
columns respectively, for some (deterministic)
\ell_{k}\in\{1,\dotsc,n_{k}-1\}
. Then, given
\mathcal{G}
, we have the following:

(a)

Q^{\top}XQ
has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})
on
\Omega_{k}
for every
k=1,\ldots,m
;

(b)

Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2}
has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k}-\ell_{k})
on
\Omega_{k}
for every
k=1,\ldots,m
;

(c)

Q^{\top}XQ_{1}
and
Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2}
are conditionally independent.

Remark 6.13
.

Note that if
n_{1}=\cdots=n_{m}=n
, then under the first condition of the lemma, it follows from Remark
7.4
that
X
has unconditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n)
and is independent of
\mathcal{G}
. Thus, in the instructive special case where
m=1
and
1\leq\ell_{1}<n=n_{1}
, the result above simplifies to the following: suppose that
X\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
, and is independent of
\mathcal{G}
, and moreover that
Q=(Q_{1}\;Q_{2})\colon(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})\to\mathbb{O}_{n}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-measurable map such that
Q_{1},Q_{2}
have
\ell_{1}
and
n-\ell_{1}
columns respectively. Then

(a)

Q^{\top}XQ\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
and is independent of
\mathcal{G}
;

(b)

Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2}\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n-\ell_{1})
and is independent of
\mathcal{G}
;

(c)

Q^{\top}XQ_{1}
and
Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2}
are independent, and also conditionally independent given
\mathcal{G}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.12
.

(a) For
\ell=1,\ldots,n
, let
\mathcal{A}_{\ell}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\ell}
be the Borel
\sigma
-algebras on
\mathscr{X}_{\ell}:=\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell}
and
\mathscr{Y}_{\ell}:=\mathbb{O}_{\ell}
respectively. Define
\phi_{\ell}\colon\mathscr{X}_{\ell}\times\mathscr{Y}_{\ell}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}_{\ell}
by
\phi_{\ell}(M,J):=J^{\top}MJ
. In the notation of Lemma
7.6
(b), the orthogonal invariance property of
\mathrm{GOE}(\ell)
can be restated as
\mathrm{GOE}(\ell)=\mathrm{GOE}(\ell)\circ(\phi_{\ell}\circ\iota_{J})^{-1}
for every
J\in\mathbb{O}_{\ell}
. Thus, observing that
\phi_{n_{k}}(X,Q)=Q^{\top}XQ
on
\Omega_{k}
, and applying Lemma
7.6
(b) to
\phi_{n_{k}}
, we see that
Q^{\top}XQ
has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})
given
\mathcal{G}
on
\Omega_{k}
, as required.

(b) For
k=1,\dotsc,m
, let
\psi_{k}\colon\mathscr{X}_{n_{k}}\rightarrow\mathscr{X}_{n_{k}-\ell_{k}}
denote the map that extracts the lower-right
(n_{k}-\ell_{k})\times(n_{k}-\ell_{k})
block of entries of an
n_{k}\times n_{k}
matrix. Then
\psi_{k}(W)\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k}-\ell_{k})
whenever
W\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})
, so
\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k}-\ell_{k})=\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})\circ\psi_{k}^{-1}=\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})\circ(\phi_{n_{k}}\circ\iota_{J})^{-1}\circ\psi_{k}^{-1}
for every
J\in\mathbb{O}_{n_{k}}
. We can therefore apply Lemma
7.6
(b) to
\psi_{k}\circ\phi_{n_{k}}
to conclude that
Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2}
has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k}-\ell_{k})
given
\mathcal{G}
on
\Omega_{k}
.

(c) For
\omega\in\Omega
, let
P_{\omega}
,
Q_{\omega}
and
R_{\omega}
respectively denote the conditional distributions of
Q^{\top}XQ_{1}
,
Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2}
and
(Q^{\top}XQ_{1},Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2})
given
\mathcal{G}
. For
k=1,\ldots,m
, let
\tilde{\psi}_{k}\colon\mathscr{X}_{n_{k}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n_{k}\times\ell_{k}}
denote the map that extracts the first
\ell_{k}
columns of a
n_{k}\times n_{k}
matrix. Now define
\Psi_{k}\colon\mathscr{X}_{n_{k}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n_{k}\times\ell_{k}}\times\mathscr{X}_{n_{k}-\ell_{k}}
by
\Psi_{k}(M):=\bigl(\tilde{\psi}_{k}(M),\psi_{k}(M)\bigr)
. Then
\tilde{\psi}_{k}(W)
and
\psi_{k}(W)
are independent whenever
W\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})
, so
\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})\circ\Psi_{k}^{-1}=\bigl(\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})\circ\tilde{\psi}_{k}^{-1}\bigr)\otimes\bigl(\mathrm{GOE}(n_{k})\circ\psi_{k}^{-1}\bigr)
. Since
(Q^{\top}XQ_{1},Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2})=(\Psi_{k}\circ\phi_{k})(X,Q)
on
\Omega_{k}
, we may apply Lemma
7.6
(b) to
\tilde{\psi}_{k}\circ\phi_{n_{k}}
,
\psi_{k}\circ\phi_{n_{k}}
and
\Psi_{k}\circ\phi_{n_{k}}
to deduce that
R_{\omega}=P_{\omega}\otimes Q_{\omega}
for all
\omega\in\Omega_{k}
. Since
k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
was arbitrary, we conclude that
R_{\omega}=P_{\omega}\otimes Q_{\omega}
for all
\omega\in\Omega=\bigsqcup_{\,k=1}^{\,m}\Omega_{k}
, which together with Lemma
7.9
(b) implies that
Q^{\top}XQ_{1}
and
Q_{2}^{\top}XQ_{2}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{G}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.8
.

We argue by induction on
k\in\{0,1,\dotsc,n-1\}
. The case
k=0
is trivial since
W\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
and is independent of
(m^{0},\gamma)
by assumption.
For a general
1\leq k\leq n-1
(when
n\geq 2
), let
\tilde{U}_{k-1}
be any
\mathscr{S}_{k-2}
-measurable
n\times(n-r_{k-1})
matrix whose columns form an orthonormal basis of
V_{k-1}
, and fix an arbitrary
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
-measurable
n\times(n-r_{k})
matrix
\tilde{U}_{k}
whose columns form an orthonormal basis of
V_{k}
. Moreover, let
E\equiv E_{k-1}
be the event
\{\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}\neq 0\}=\{r_{k}=r_{k-1}+1\}\in\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
, and note that
n-r_{k-1}\geq n-k+1\geq 2
.

Next, define an
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
-measurable
n\times(n-r_{k-1})
matrix
\check{U}
by setting
\check{U}:=(\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}\;\tilde{U}_{k})
on
E
and
\check{U}:=\tilde{U}_{k}
on
E^{c}=\{\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}=0\}
. Letting
\breve{U}
be the
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
-measurable
n\times(n-r_{k-1}-1)
matrix obtained by removing the first column
\check{U}e_{1}
of
\check{U}
, we therefore have
\tilde{U}_{k}=\breve{U}
on
E
and
\tilde{U}_{k}=\check{U}
on
E^{c}
. Now
\check{U},\tilde{U}_{k-1}
have orthonormal columns that span
V_{k-1}
, so
Q:=\tilde{U}_{k-1}^{\top}\check{U}
is an
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
-measurable orthogonal
(n-r_{k-1})\times(n-r_{k-1})
matrix such that
\check{U}^{\top}W\check{U}=Q^{\top}\bigl(\tilde{U}_{k-1}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k-1}\bigr)Q
.
By the inductive hypothesis,
\tilde{U}_{k-1}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k-1}
has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n-r_{k-1})
given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
, so it follows from parts (a) and (b) respectively of Lemma
6.12
(with
\ell\equiv 1
and
N=n-r_{k-1}\geq 2
) that
\check{U}^{\top}W\check{U}
and
\breve{U}^{\top}W\breve{U}
have conditional distributions
\mathrm{GOE}(n-r_{k-1})
and
\mathrm{GOE}(n-r_{k-1}-1)
respectively given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
. Since
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}=\check{U}^{\top}W\check{U}
on
E^{c}=\{r_{k}=r_{k-1}\}\in\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
and
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}=\breve{U}^{\top}W\breve{U}
on
E=\{r_{k}=r_{k-1}+1\}\in\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
, we deduce from Lemma
7.6
(a) that
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}
has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n-r_{k})
given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
, as required.

In addition, it holds trivially that 0 and
\check{U}^{\top}W\check{U}
are conditionally independent given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
, and Lemma
6.12
(c) implies that
\check{U}^{\top}W(\check{U}e_{1})=Q^{\top}(\tilde{U}_{k-1}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k-1}\bigr)Qe_{1}
and
\breve{U}^{\top}W\breve{U}
are also conditionally independent given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
. Since
W\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}=0
on
E^{c}
and
\check{U}e_{1}=\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}
on
E
, an application of Lemma
7.9
(a) shows that
\check{U}^{\top}W\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}
(and hence
\sigma(\mathscr{S}_{k-1},\check{U}^{\top}W\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}})
by Lemma
7.8
) is conditionally independent of
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}
given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
. Moreover,
WP_{k-1}=WM_{k-1}M_{k-1}^{+}=Y_{k-1}M_{k-1}^{+}
,
m^{k-1}
,
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}
,
\check{U}
and
P_{k-1}^{\perp}=\check{U}\check{U}^{\top}
are
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
-measurable, so

\displaystyle y^{k-1}=W\bigl(P_{k-1}+P_{k-1}^{\perp}\bigr)m^{k-1}

\displaystyle=(WP_{k-1})m^{k-1}+\bigl(P_{k-1}+P_{k-1}^{\perp}\bigr)^{\top}W\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}

\displaystyle=(WP_{k-1})m^{k-1}+(WP_{k-1})^{\top}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}+\check{U}\bigl(\check{U}^{\top}W\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}}\bigr)

(132)

is measurable with respect to
\sigma(\mathscr{S}_{k-1},\check{U}^{\top}W\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k-1$}})
. Thus, given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
, we conclude that
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}
is conditionally independent of
y^{k-1}
, and hence conditionally independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}=\sigma(\mathscr{S}_{k-1},y^{k-1})
by Lemma
7.8
and (
125
). Therefore, since
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}
has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n-r_{k})
given
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
, it also has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n-r_{k})
given
\sigma(\mathscr{S}_{k-1},\mathscr{S}_{k})=\mathscr{S}_{k}
.

Finally, it remains to show that if
\tilde{W}\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}
, then
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{W}\tilde{U}_{k}
also has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n-r_{k})
given
\mathscr{S}_{k}
. To see this, let
\tilde{m}^{1},\dotsc,\tilde{m}^{r_{k}}
be an
\mathscr{S}_{k}
-measurable orthonormal basis of
\Img(M_{k})
, obtained for example by applying the Gram–Schmidt procedure to
m^{0},\dotsc,m^{k-1}
, as in Remark
6.10
above. Then taking
Q_{1}=\bigl(\tilde{m}^{1}\;\cdots\;\tilde{m}^{r_{k}}\bigr)
and
Q_{2}=\tilde{U}_{k}
, we see that
Q=(Q_{1}\;Q_{2})
satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma
6.12
with
\Omega_{j}=\{r_{k}=j\}\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
and
\ell_{j}=j\leq n=n_{j}
for
j=1,\dotsc,k
. The desired conclusion now follows directly from Lemma
6.12
(b), and this completes the inductive step.
∎

As mentioned above, the proof of Proposition
6.11
relies crucially on the final assertion in Proposition
6.8
. To obtain the conditional distributional equalities in (
127
) and (
129
), we will also apply the following elementary fact.

Lemma 6.14
.

If
W\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
and
u\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is fixed, then
Wu\overset{d}{=}\|u\|_{n}Z+\zeta u
, where
Z\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
and
\zeta\sim N(0,1/n)
are independent.

Proof of Lemma
6.14
.

The result holds trivially when
u=0
, and is also true when
u=e_{1}
since
We_{1}\sim N_{n}\bigl(0,\diag(2/n,1/n,\dotsc,1/n)\bigr)
. For a general
u\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\}
, let
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be an orthogonal matrix with
Qe_{1}=u/\|u\|
, so that
Q^{\top}u=\|u\|e_{1}
. Then

Wu\overset{d}{=}QWQ^{\top}u=Q(We_{1})\|u\|\overset{d}{=}Q(\|e_{1}\|_{n}Z+\zeta e_{1})\|u\|\overset{d}{=}\|u\|_{n}Z+\zeta u,

as required, where we have used the orthogonal invariance of
W\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
, the result for
e_{1}
and the orthogonal invariance of
Z\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
respectively to obtain the distributional equalities above.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.11
.

We start by proving (
128
) for every
k\in\{0,1,\dotsc,n-1\}
. Let
\tilde{U}_{k}
be any
\mathscr{S}_{k-1}
-measurable
n\times(n-r_{k})
matrix whose columns form an orthonormal basis of
V_{k}
; see Remark
6.10
for a specific construction of
\tilde{U}_{k}
. Similarly to (
132
) in the proof of Proposition
6.8
, we can write

\displaystyle W=WP_{k}

\displaystyle+\bigl(P_{k}+P_{k}^{\perp}\bigr)^{\top}WP_{k}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=WP_{k}

\displaystyle+(WP_{k})^{\top}P_{k}^{\perp}+P_{k}^{\perp}WP_{k}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=WP_{k}

\displaystyle+(WP_{k})^{\top}P_{k}^{\perp}+\tilde{U}_{k}\bigl(\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}\bigr)\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}

(133)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{k}}}WP_{k}

\displaystyle+(WP_{k})^{\top}P_{k}^{\perp}+\tilde{U}_{k}\bigl(\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{W}^{k}\tilde{U}_{k}\bigr)\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}=WP_{k}+(WP_{k})^{\top}P_{k}^{\perp}+P_{k}^{\perp}\tilde{W}^{k}P_{k}^{\perp},

where
\tilde{W}^{k}\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}
. To justify the key distributional equality after (
133
), we can apply Lemma
7.6
(c); indeed, note that
WP_{k}=Y_{k}M_{k}^{+}
,
\tilde{U}_{k}
and
P_{k}^{\perp}=\tilde{U}_{k}\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}
are
\mathscr{S}_{k}
-measurable, and that
\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}W\tilde{U}_{k}\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{k}}}\tilde{U}_{k}^{\top}\tilde{W}^{k}\tilde{U}_{k}
by the final assertion of Proposition
6.8
. By replacing
WP_{k}
with
Y_{k}M_{k}^{+}
in the display above, we obtain (
128
) for every
k\in\{0,1,\dotsc,n-1\}
, as desired. Since
I-P_{0}=P_{0}^{\perp}=I_{n}
, this specialises to
W\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{0}}}\tilde{W}^{0}
when
k=0
, which is the first part of (
127
).

Using (
128
), we now derive the conditional distribution of
h^{k+1}
given
\mathscr{S}_{k}
for
k\in\{0,1,\dotsc,n-1\}
. When
k=0
, we have
h^{1}=Wm^{0}
, so the associated identity in (
127
) follows directly from the first part of (
127
), Lemma
6.14
and Lemma
7.6
(c). Turning now to (
129
) with
k\geq 1
, we have
h^{k+1}=Wm^{k}-b_{k}m^{k-1}
, where
b_{k},m^{k-1}
are
\mathscr{S}_{k}
-measurable, so we can deduce from (
128
) and Lemma
7.6
(c) that

\displaystyle h^{k+1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{k}}}Y_{k}M_{k}^{+}m^{k}+(Y_{k}M_{k}^{+})^{\top}P_{k}^{\perp}m^{k}+P_{k}^{\perp}\tilde{W}^{k}P_{k}^{\perp}m^{k}-b_{k}m^{k-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{\left.=\right|}=Y_{k}\alpha^{k}+(Y_{k}M_{k}^{+})^{\top}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}+P_{k}^{\perp}(\tilde{W}^{k}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}})-b_{k}m^{k-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{\left.=\right|}=H_{k}\alpha^{k}+(0\;M_{k-1})B_{k}\alpha^{k}+(H_{k}M_{k}^{+})^{\top}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}+P_{k}^{\perp}(\tilde{W}^{k}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}})-b_{k}m^{k-1}.

(134)

Indeed, to obtain the final equality above, observe that
Y_{k}=H_{k}+(0\;M_{k-1})B_{k}
and
B_{k}^{\top}(0\;M_{k-1})^{\top}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}=B_{k}^{\top}(0\;M_{k-1})^{\top}P_{k}^{\perp}m^{k}=0
in view of the fact that
P_{k}^{\perp}M_{k-1}=0
. Since
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}
is
\mathscr{S}_{k}
-measurable and
\tilde{W}^{k}\sim\mathrm{GOE}(n)
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}
(and therefore has conditional distribution
\mathrm{GOE}(n)
given
\mathscr{S}_{k}
), it follows from Lemmas
6.14
and
7.6
(b) that
\tilde{W}^{k}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{k}}}\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}+\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}
. Now since
P_{k}^{\perp}
and all the other summands in (
134
) are
\mathscr{S}_{k}
-measurable, a further application of Lemma
7.6
(c) shows that the random variable in (
134
) and

\displaystyle H_{k}\alpha^{k}+P_{k}^{\perp}\bigl(\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}+\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\bigr)+(H_{k}M_{k}^{+})^{\top}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}-\bigl\{b_{k}m^{k-1}-(0\;M_{k-1})B_{k}\alpha^{k}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,h^{\ell}+P_{k}^{\perp}\bigl(\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}+\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\bigr)+(H_{k}M_{k}^{+})^{\top}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}-\biggl(b_{k}m^{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,b_{\ell-1}m^{\ell-2}\biggr)

(135)

are identically distributed given
\mathscr{S}_{k}
. Finally, recall that
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}=(I-P_{k})\,m^{k}=m^{k}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,m^{\ell-1}
, and that
(M_{k}^{+})^{\top}M_{k}^{\top}m^{\ell}=P_{k}^{\top}m^{\ell}=P_{k}m^{\ell}=m^{\ell}
for all
0\leq\ell\leq k-1
by the definition of the projection matrix
P_{k}=M_{k}M_{k}^{+}
. It follows that
P_{k}^{\perp}(\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}})=\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}
and

\displaystyle(M_{k}^{+})^{\top}H_{k}^{\top}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}

\displaystyle=(M_{k}^{+})^{\top}\biggl(H_{k}^{\top}m^{k}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,H_{k}^{\top}m^{\ell-1}\biggr)

\displaystyle b_{k}m^{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell-1}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,m^{\ell-2}

\displaystyle=(M_{k}^{+})^{\top}\biggl(b_{k}\,M_{k}^{\top}m^{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,b_{\ell-1}\,M_{k}^{\top}m^{\ell-2}\biggr).

Thus, since
(M_{k}^{+})^{\top}=M_{k}(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k})^{+}
, the random variable
h^{k+1,k}
defined in (
129
) is identical to that in (
135
), so we conclude from (
134
) that
h^{k+1}\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{k}}}h^{k+1,k}
, as required.
∎

6.4
Proof outline for the AMP master theorems in Section
2.1

Recalling the definition (
10
) of the limiting covariance matrices
\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
in Theorem
2.3
, we first outline a standard construction of a single random sequence
(\bar{G}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N})
satisfying
(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k})\sim N_{k}(0,\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]})
for each
k
. Let
\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1}:=(\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{1,k+1},\dotsc,\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k,k+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and

\bar{\alpha}^{k}\equiv(\bar{\alpha}_{1}^{k},\dotsc,\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{k}):=\bigl(\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\bigr)^{-1}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}

(136)

for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, where the latter is well-defined since
\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}
is positive definite under
(A4)
by Lemma
2.2
. It is easily verified that if
(G_{1},\dotsc,G_{k+1})\sim N_{k+1}(0,\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k+1]})
, then
G_{[k]}:=(G_{1},\dotsc,G_{k})
and
\xi_{k+1}:=G_{k+1}-G_{[k]}^{\top}\,\bar{\alpha}^{k}=G_{k+1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,G_{\ell}
are uncorrelated and hence independent. This means that

G_{[k]}^{\top}\,\bar{\alpha}^{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,G_{\ell}=\mathbb{E}(G_{k+1}\,|\,G_{1},\dotsc,G_{k})

for each
k
. Moreover, since
\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{k+1}=\Cov(G_{1},\dotsc,G_{k+1})
is positive definite and
\xi_{k+1}
is a non-trivial linear combination of
G_{1},\dotsc,G_{k+1}
, it follows under
(A4)
that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<\Var(\xi_{k+1})=\Var(\xi_{k+1}\,|\,G_{1},\dotsc,G_{k})=\Var(G_{k+1}\,|\,G_{1},\dotsc,G_{k})

\displaystyle=\Var(G_{k+1})-\Var\bigl(G_{[k]}^{\top}\,\bar{\alpha}^{k}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k+1,k+1}-(\bar{\alpha}^{k})^{\top}\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\,\bar{\alpha}^{k}

\displaystyle=\tau_{k+1}^{2}-(\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1})^{\top}\bigl(\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\bigr)^{-1}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1}=:\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}_{k+1}

(137)

for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, so that
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\in(0,\infty)
satisfies
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}_{k+1}=\Var(\xi_{k+1})\leq\Var(G_{k+1})=\tau_{k+1}^{2}
.
Now let
\bar{G}_{1}\sim N(0,\tau_{1}^{2})
, and for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, inductively define

\bar{G}_{k+1}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,\bar{G}_{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\zeta}_{k+1},

(138)

where
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\zeta}_{k+1}\sim N(0,1)
is independent of
(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k})
. Then
(\bar{G}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N})
is a random sequence with
(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k})\sim N_{k}(0,\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]})
for each
k
, as desired.
With the above definitions in place, we record here some key identities.
In view of (
10
), we certainly have

\Cov(\bar{G}_{k},\bar{G}_{\ell})=\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{k}\bar{G}_{\ell})=\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k,\ell}=\begin{cases}\,\tau_{1}^{2}\quad&\text{if }k=\ell=1\\
\,\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot f_{k-1}(\bar{G}_{k-1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)\quad&\text{if }k>\ell=1\\
\,\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{\ell-1}(\bar{G}_{\ell-1},\bar{\gamma})\cdot f_{k-1}(\bar{G}_{k-1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)\quad&\text{if }k\geq\ell\geq 2,\end{cases}

(139)

where
f_{1},f_{2},\dotsc
are the Lipschitz functions in the AMP recursion (
5
), and
\tau_{1}
and
F_{0}
are as in
(A2)
and
(A3)
respectively. This fact underlies an important assertion (Proposition
6.16
(e) below) in our inductive proof of the master theorems. Moreover, for
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and any Lipschitz function
\varphi\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
with weak derivative
\varphi^{\prime}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bar{G}_{k}\,\varphi(\bar{G}_{\ell})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell})\bigr)\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{k}\bar{G}_{\ell})=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell})\bigr)\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k,\ell}.

(140)

This follows from Stein’s lemma, a general formulation of which can be found in
Tsybakov 2009
and Lemma
6.20
.

Lemma 6.15

(Stein’s lemma)
.

If
Z\sim N(0,\sigma^{2})
and
\varphi\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is an absolutely continuous function with weak derivative
\varphi^{\prime}
such that
\varphi^{\prime}(Z)
is integrable, then
\mathbb{E}\bigl(Z\varphi(Z)\bigr)=\sigma^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl(\varphi^{\prime}(Z)\bigr)
.

Indeed, the first equality in (
140
) follows from Lemma
6.15
upon writing
\bar{G}_{k}=(\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k,\ell}/\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{\ell,\ell})\,\bar{G}_{\ell}+\xi_{k\ell}
, where
\xi_{k\ell}
has zero mean and is independent of
\bar{G}_{\ell}
, so that
\mathbb{E}\bigl(\xi_{k\ell}\,\varphi(\bar{G}_{\ell})\bigr)=0
.

Our choice of functions
(f_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
and
(f_{k}^{\prime})_{k=0}^{\infty}
in (
5
) ensures that for any fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}
, we can take
\varphi=f_{\ell}(\cdot\,,y)
and
\varphi^{\prime}=f_{\ell}^{\prime}(\cdot\,,y)
in (
140
) to see that
\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bar{G}_{k}\,f_{\ell}(\bar{G}_{\ell},y)\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{\ell}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell},y)\bigr)\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{k}\bar{G}_{\ell})
for
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
We deduce from this (and Lemma
7.7
) that if
\bar{\gamma}\sim\pi
is independent of
\bar{G}_{1},\bar{G}_{2},\dotsc
, then

\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bar{G}_{k}\,f_{\ell}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{\ell}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{k}\bar{G}_{\ell})=\bar{b}_{\ell}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k,\ell}

(141)

for all
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\bar{b}_{\ell}:=\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{\ell}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
. This forms part of assertion (f) in Proposition
6.16
below.

To complete our technical preparations for the main derivations below, we will set up a more explicit connection between the Gaussian variables
\bar{G}_{k+1}\sim N(0,\tau_{k+1}^{2})
in (
138
) and the random vectors
h^{k+1,k}\equiv h^{k+1,k}(n)
defined for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\in\{0,1,\dotsc,n-1\}
in (
127
) and (
129
) in Section
6.2
above. For such
n
and
k
, Proposition
6.11
asserts that
h^{k+1}(n)
and
h^{k+1,k}(n)
are identically distributed given
\mathscr{S}_{k}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{k}(n)=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0},h^{j}:1\leq j\leq k)
, and we now write
h^{k+1,k}(n)=\tilde{h}^{k+1}(n)+\Delta^{k+1}(n)
, where

\tilde{h}^{1}\equiv\tilde{h}^{1}(n):=\tau_{1}\tilde{Z}^{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\Delta^{1}\equiv\Delta^{1}(n):=(\|m^{0}\|_{n}-\tau_{1})\tilde{Z}^{1}+\tilde{\zeta}^{1}m^{0},

(142)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{k+1}\equiv\tilde{h}^{k+1}(n)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,h^{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\tilde{Z}^{k+1},

(143)

\displaystyle\Delta^{k+1}\equiv\Delta^{k+1}(n)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}(\alpha_{\ell}^{k}-\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k})\,h^{\ell}+M_{k}(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k})^{+}\biggl(v^{k,k}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,v^{k,\ell-1}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 32.72049pt-\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}(P_{k}\tilde{Z}^{k+1})+(\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}-\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1})\tilde{Z}^{k+1}+\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}.

(144)

Recall that
(\tilde{Z}^{k+1},\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1})\equiv\bigl(\tilde{Z}^{k+1}(n),\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}(n)\bigr)\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})\otimes N(0,1/n)
was taken to be independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{k}(n)
in Proposition
6.11
, where we also defined
\alpha^{k}\equiv\alpha^{k}(n)
and
v^{k,\ell}\equiv v^{k,\ell}(n)
for
0\leq\ell\leq k
.

In the decomposition above, we have defined
\tilde{h}^{k+1}
in (
143
) to mimic the expression for the limiting Gaussian variable
\bar{G}_{k+1}
in (
138
).
Contrasting the definitions of
h^{k+1,k}
and
\tilde{h}^{k+1}
in (
129
) and (
143
) respectively for
k\in\{0,1,\dotsc,n-1\}
, we see that the random quantities
\alpha^{k}
and
\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}
in (
129
) are replaced in (
143
) with the deterministic
\bar{\alpha}^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\in(0,\infty)
from (
136
) and (
137
) respectively; these turn out to be the correct limiting values in Proposition
6.16
(i, j) below under the non-degeneracy assumption
(A4)
.

We are now in a position to state the main result of this subsection. To ease notation, we will often suppress the dependence on
n
of quantities such as
h^{k}\equiv h^{k}(n)
,
v^{k,\ell}\equiv v^{k,\ell}(n)
,
\alpha^{k}\equiv\alpha^{k}(n)
and
\Delta^{k}\equiv\Delta^{k}(n)
.

Proposition 6.16
.

For a sequence of symmetric AMP recursions (
5
) satisfying
(A0)
–
(A5)
as well as
(A4)
, the following hold as
n\to\infty
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
:

(a)

\|\Delta^{k}\|_{n,r}\overset{c}{\to}0
;

(b)

\|h^{j}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
for
1\leq j\leq k
;

\|m^{j}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
for
0\leq j\leq k
;

(c)

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
for every
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(r)
;

(d)

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\,\phi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot\phi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
for every
\phi\in\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(1)
;

(e)

\langle m^{j-1},m^{\ell-1}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{j}\bar{G}_{\ell})=\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}
for
1\leq j,\ell\leq k+1
;

(f)

\langle h^{j},m^{\ell}\rangle_{n}=\langle h^{j},f_{\ell}(h^{\ell},\gamma)\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bar{G}_{j}f_{\ell}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{\ell}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{j}\bar{G}_{\ell})=\bar{b}_{\ell}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}
for
1\leq j,\ell\leq k
;

\langle h^{j},m^{0}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}0
for
1\leq j\leq k
;

(g)

b_{k}=\langle f_{k}^{\prime}(h^{k},\gamma)\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{k}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\bar{b}_{k}
;

(h)

v^{k,\ell}/n=(H_{k}^{\top}m^{\ell}-b_{\ell}\,M_{k}^{\top}m^{\ell-1})/n\overset{c}{\to}0
for
0\leq\ell\leq k
;

(i)

\alpha^{k}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\alpha}^{k}
;

(j)

\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}=\Var^{1/2}(\bar{G}_{k+1}\,|\,\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k})
.

Remark 6.17
.

Under
(A4)
and the alternative hypotheses of Remark
6.1
(a), the assertions (a)–(j) above remain valid if we replace
\overset{c}{\to}
with
\overset{p}{\to}
and
O_{c}(1)
with
O_{p}(1)
throughout.

To establish Proposition
6.16
, we proceed by induction on
k\in\mathbb{N}
and prove the assertions (a)–(i) one at a time (in that order). Here, we will give a technical summary of the inductive argument (which can be read alongside the detailed proof in Section
6.5
) to highlight its overall structure and key features. Henceforth, we write
\mathcal{H}_{k}(\cdots)
for parts
(\cdots)
of the inductive hypothesis for
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

\mathcal{H}_{k}(e,f)
: These are obtained as direct consequences of the inductive hypotheses
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c,d)
by choosing suitable pseudo-Lipschitz functions
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(2)\subseteq\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(r)
that depend on at most three of their
k+1
arguments. We use
\mathcal{H}_{k}(d)
to handle the inner products that feature
m^{0}
and apply
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
to those that do not. In
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
, the limiting value of
\langle m^{j-1},m^{\ell-1}\rangle_{n}=\langle f_{j-1}(h^{j-1},\gamma),f_{\ell-1}(h^{\ell-1},\gamma)\rangle_{n}
is shown to be

\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{j-1}(\bar{G}_{j-1},\bar{\gamma})\cdot f_{\ell-1}(\bar{G}_{\ell-1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{j}\bar{G}_{\ell})\quad&\text{for }2\leq j,\ell\leq k+1\\
\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot f_{j-1}(\bar{G}_{j-1},\bar{\gamma}))=\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{1}\bar{G}_{\ell})\quad&\text{for }1=j<\ell\leq k+1,\end{cases}

where the two equalities are drawn from (
139
) and form the basis of the definition of the limiting covariances
\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}
in (
10
). Moreover, for
1\leq j,\ell\leq k
, the identity
\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bar{G}_{j}f_{\ell}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{\ell}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{j}\bar{G}_{\ell})
in the first line of
\mathcal{H}_{k}(f)
comes from (
141
). These identities (
139
) and (
141
) ultimately provide the crucial link between the limiting values of
\langle h^{j},m^{\ell}\rangle_{n}
and
b_{\ell}\,\langle m^{j-1},m^{\ell-1}\rangle_{n}
in
\mathcal{H}_{k}(h)
.

\mathcal{H}_{k}(g,h)
: This is also derived from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
, but since
f_{k}^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
need not lie in
\mathrm{PL}_{2}(r)
, we instead apply the analytic Lemmas
7.10
and
7.14
rather than imitate the proofs of
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e,f)
. See the proof of Corollary
7.21
(b) for a similar argument.
\mathcal{H}_{k}(h)
follows immediately from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e,f,g)
.

\mathcal{H}_{k}(i,j)
: We see from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
that the matrices
M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
converge completely to the limiting covariance matrix
\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}=\Cov(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, which is positive definite under
(A4)
.
In
\mathcal{H}_{k}(i)
, we consider
\alpha^{k}=(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)^{+}(M_{k}^{\top}m^{k}/n)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, a vector of projection coefficients defined in (
126
).
It follows from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
that
(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)^{+}
and
M_{k}^{\top}m^{k}/n
converge completely to
(\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]})^{-1}
and
\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1}
respectively, and hence that
\alpha^{k}\overset{c}{\to}(\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]})^{-1}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1}=\bar{\alpha}^{k}
, as defined in (
136
). For
\mathcal{H}_{k}(j)
, we recall the definitions at the start of Section
6.2
and write

\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}^{2}=\|P_{k}^{\perp}m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}=\|m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}-\|P_{k}m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}=\|m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}-(\alpha^{k})^{\top}(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)\,\alpha^{k}.

Applying
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e,i)
to the individual terms on the right hand side above,
we deduce that
\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}^{2}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k+1,k+1}-(\bar{\alpha}^{k})^{\top}\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\,\bar{\alpha}^{k}=\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}_{k+1}
, as defined in (
137
).

\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(a)
:
It is thanks to the key fact
\mathcal{H}_{k}(h)
and the presence of the Onsager term
-b_{k}m^{k-1}
in the original AMP recursion (
5
) (and subsequently in (
129
) in Proposition
6.11
) that the
\|{\cdot}\|_{n,r}
norm of the second term in (
144
)
converges completely to 0.
Using
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b,i,j)
to handle some of the remaining terms in this definition (
144
) of the deviation term
\Delta^{k+1}
, we conclude that
\|\Delta^{k+1}\|_{n,r}\overset{c}{\to}0
.

\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(b)
: Using the distributional equality
h^{k+1}\overset{d}{=}h^{k+1,k}=\tilde{h}^{k+1}+\Delta^{k+1}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,h^{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}+\Delta^{k+1}
from Proposition
6.11
and (
143
,
144
), we deduce from
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(a)
and the inductive hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b)
that
\|h^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
. Since
\|\gamma\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
by
(A1)
and
f_{k+1}
is Lipschitz, this in turn implies that
\|m^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=\|f_{k}(h^{k+1},\gamma)\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
.

\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c)
: This is the main assertion in Proposition
6.16
; by Corollary
7.21
(b), it is in fact equivalent to the conclusion (
12
) of Theorem
2.3
. We first condition on
\mathscr{S}_{k}=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0},h^{j}:1\leq j\leq k)
and appeal to Proposition
6.11
, which asserts that for each
n>k
, the conditional distribution of
h^{k+1}\equiv h^{k+1}(n)
given
\mathscr{S}_{k}
is identical to that of
h^{k+1,k}\equiv h^{k+1,k}(n)
from (
129
). With
h^{k+1,k}=\tilde{h}^{k+1}+\Delta^{k+1}
in place of
h^{k+1}
on the left hand side of
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c)
, we use
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(a,b)
to show that the ‘deviation’ term
\Delta^{k+1}\equiv\Delta^{k+1}(n)
from (
144
) has asymptotically negligible effect, so that
h^{k+1,k}
can in fact be replaced with
\tilde{h}^{k+1}
in all relevant expressions. In (
143
),
\tilde{h}^{k+1}
was defined as
\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,h^{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}
, where
\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,h^{\ell}
is a deterministic linear combination of the previous iterates
h^{1},\dotsc,h^{k}
, and
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}
is a new Gaussian variable that has i.i.d. components and is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}
.

In view of this, the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c)
can be completed in two stages (given by (
164
) and (
162
) below): the influence of the latter Gaussian term can first be understood by appealing to
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(b)
and a general concentration result for sums of pseudo-Lipschitz functions of independent Gaussians (Lemma
7.12
), before we subsequently reintroduce the randomness in
\gamma,m^{0},h^{1},\dotsc,h^{k}
and apply the inductive hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
to account for this.
The appearance of the new limiting Gaussian variable
\bar{G}_{k+1}
on the right hand side of
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c)
(in addition to the existing
\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k}
from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
) can be explained through its definition in (
138
), which matches up neatly with the definition (
143
) of
\tilde{h}^{k+1}
and the two-stage argument we have just outlined; see (
162
) and (
163
) in the proof.

\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(d)
: The proof of this is similar in spirit to that of
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c)
, except that it also makes use of condition
(A3)
. Note also that
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(d)
applies only to Lipschitz
\phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{k+2}\to\mathbb{R}
rather than general
\phi\in\mathrm{PL}_{k+2}(r)
, but this is sufficient for our purposes in the subsequent proofs of
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(e,f)
.

The proofs we give for
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c,d)
combine aspects of the asymptotic and finite-sample arguments (see Remark
6.3
) in the existing AMP literature. Proposition E.1 in
Fan 2020
provides the basis for an alternative asymptotic approach, whose details we omit.

6.5
Proofs for Sections
2.1
and
6.1

Proof of Proposition
6.16
.

Since we are carrying out an asymptotic analysis, we may assume without loss of generality that
n>k
in the proofs of
\mathcal{H}_{k}(a,\dotsc,i)
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
; this enables us to apply the results on conditional distributions from Section
6.2
. Note also that we use
T_{n},T_{n1},T_{n1}^{\prime},T_{n2}
to refer to different quantities of interest in different parts of the proof. In Lemma
7.2
and Remark
7.3
, we state versions of the continuous mapping theorem and Slutsky’s lemma for complete convergence, as well the ‘arithmetic rules’ for
o_{c}
and
O_{c}
symbols. We will apply these repeatedly in the arguments below, often without further comment or explanation.

First, we prove
\mathcal{H}_{1}(a,b,c,d)
, which form the base case for the induction.

\mathcal{H}_{1}(a)
: Recall from (
142
) that
\Delta^{1}\equiv\Delta^{1}(n)=(\|m^{0}\|_{n}-\tau_{1})\tilde{Z}^{1}+\tilde{\zeta}^{1}m^{0}
, where
(\tilde{Z}^{1},\tilde{\zeta}^{1})\equiv(\tilde{Z}^{1}(n),\tilde{\zeta}^{1}(n))\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})\otimes N(0,1/n)
for each
n
. Taking
\zeta\sim N(0,1)
, we have
\lvert\tilde{\zeta}^{1}\rvert\overset{d}{=}n^{-1/2}\,\lvert\zeta\rvert\overset{c}{\to}0
by Example
1
(a), and
\|\tilde{Z}^{1}\|_{n,r}=(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\lvert\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1}\rvert^{r})^{1/r}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}(\lvert\zeta\rvert^{r})^{1/r}\in(0,\infty)
by Lemma
7.12
and Proposition
1.2
. Moreover,
\bigl|\|m^{0}\|_{n}-\tau_{1}\bigr|\overset{c}{\to}0
and
\|m^{0}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
by
(A2)
. Putting everything together, we recall from Remark
7.3
the ‘arithmetic rules’ (
190
) for
o_{c}
and
O_{c}
symbols, and conclude using the triangle inequality for
\|{\cdot}\|_{n,r}
that

\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}\leq\bigl|\|m^{0}\|_{n}-\tau_{1}\bigr|\,\|\tilde{Z}^{1}\|_{n,r}+\lvert\tilde{\zeta}^{1}\rvert\,\|m^{0}\|_{n,r}=o_{c}(1)\,O_{c}(1)+o_{c}(1)\,O_{c}(1)=o_{c}(1).

\mathcal{H}_{1}(b)
: Recall from (
127
) in Proposition
6.11
and (
142
) that

h^{1}\equiv h^{1}(n)\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{0}}}\tilde{h}^{1}(n)+\Delta^{1}(n)=h^{1,0}(n)\equiv h^{1,0}

(145)

for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\tilde{h}^{1}\equiv\tilde{h}^{1}(n)=\tau_{1}\tilde{Z}^{1}
and
\tilde{Z}^{1}\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{0}=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0})
. Then
\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}=o_{c}(1)
by
\mathcal{H}_{1}(a)
and
\|\tilde{h}^{1}\|_{n,r}=\tau_{1}\|\tilde{Z}^{1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
as in the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(a)
, so

\|h^{1}\|_{n,r}\overset{d}{=}\|h^{1,0}\|_{n,r}\leq\|\tilde{h}^{1}\|_{n,r}+\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)+o_{c}(1)=O_{c}(1).

We already have
\|m^{0}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
by
(A2)
. In addition,
\|\gamma\|_{n,r}=(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\lvert\gamma_{i}\rvert^{r})^{1/r}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}(\lvert\bar{\gamma}\rvert^{r})^{1/r}
by
(A1)
, so
\|\gamma\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
. Letting
L^{\prime}>0
be such that the function
f_{1}
in the AMP recursion (
5
) lies in
\mathrm{PL}_{2}(1,L^{\prime})
, we have
\lvert f_{1}(x,y)\rvert\leq\lvert f_{1}(0,0)\rvert+L^{\prime}(\lvert x\rvert+\lvert y\rvert)
for all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, so we can apply the triangle inequality for
\|{\cdot}\|_{n,r}
to deduce that

\|m^{1}\|_{n,r}=\|f_{1}(h^{1},\gamma)\|_{n,r}\leq\lvert f_{1}(0,0)\rvert\,\|\mathbf{1}_{n}\|_{n,r}+L^{\prime}(\|h^{1}\|_{n,r}+\|\gamma\|_{n,r})=O_{c}(1).

\mathcal{H}_{1}(c)
: For each
n
, note that
(\gamma,h^{1})\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{0}}}(\gamma,h^{1,0})
by (
145
) and Lemma
7.6
(c). Thus, for each fixed
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(r)
, it follows that
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(h_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\overset{d}{=}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(h_{i}^{1,0},\gamma_{i})=:T_{n}
for each
n
, so in view of the third bullet point in Remark
6.1
, it is enough to show that
T_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
as
n\to\infty
. To this end, we write

T_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\bigl\{\psi(h_{i}^{1,0},\gamma_{i})-\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\bigr\}=:T_{n1}+T_{n2}

for each
n
, and aim to prove that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
and
T_{n2}\overset{c}{\to}0
, which together imply the desired conclusion.

Before proceeding, we briefly describe the techniques that we use to determine the limit of
(T_{n1})
and also to prove
\mathcal{H}_{1}(d)
and
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c,d)
later on. It is instructive to consider the following two special cases where the claim is easier to establish. If
\psi
depends only on its first argument, then since
h^{1}(n)=\tau_{1}\tilde{Z}^{1}(n)
and
\tilde{Z}^{1}(n)\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
for each
n
, the result follows readily from the concentration inequality (
195
) in Lemma
7.12
and the characterisation of complete convergence in Proposition
1.2
. On the other hand, if
\psi
depends only on its second argument, then since
\bigl(\gamma\equiv\gamma(n):n\in\mathbb{N}\bigr)
satisfies
(A1)
by assumption, we can appeal directly to Corollary
7.21
(b).

For general
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(r)
, we seek to combine these two different lines of reasoning by exploiting the independence of
\tilde{h}^{1}(n)
and
\mathscr{S}_{0}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0})
for each
n
. This allows
\gamma(n)
and
\tilde{h}^{1}(n)
to be handled separately (to a large extent) when we decompose
T_{n1}
as a sum of
\mathbb{E}(T_{n1}\,|\,\mathscr{S}_{0})
and
T_{n1}-\mathbb{E}(T_{n1}\,|\,\mathscr{S}_{0})
in (
146
) and (
147
) respectively. For the latter, it is helpful to first think of
\gamma(n)
as being fixed when applying Lemma
7.12
to the Gaussian
\tilde{h}^{1}
, before subsequently accounting for the randomness of
\gamma(n)
using
(A1)
.

Define
\Psi\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\Psi(y):=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\tau_{1}Z,y)\bigr)
with
Z\sim N(0,1)
. For each
n
, since
\tilde{Z}^{1}\equiv\tilde{Z}^{1}(n)\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{0}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0})
, we deduce from Lemma
7.7
that
\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\tau_{1}\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\Psi(\gamma_{i})
almost surely, for every
1\leq i\leq n
. Since
\Psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{1}(r)
by Lemma
7.23
(b), it follows from
(A1)
and Corollary
7.21
(b) that
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Psi(\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\Psi(\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
as
n\to\infty
, where
\bar{\gamma}\sim\pi
. A further application of Lemma
7.7
shows that if
\bar{G}_{1}\sim N(0,\tau_{1}^{2})
is independent of
\bar{\gamma}
, then
\mathbb{E}\bigl(\Psi(\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\!\bigm|\!\bar{\gamma}\bigr\}\bigl)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
, so in summary, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Psi(\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\Psi(\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr).

(146)

To complete the proof that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
, we must therefore show that

T_{n1}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\bigl\{\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)\bigr\}\overset{c}{\to}0

(147)

as
n\to\infty
. To this end, let
L>0
be such that
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(r,L)
, and for each
y\in\mathbb{R}
, define
\psi_{y},\bar{\psi}_{y}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\psi_{y}(z):=\psi(\tau_{1}z,y)
and
\bar{\psi}_{y}(z):=\psi_{y}(z)-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi_{y}(Z)\bigr)
, where
Z\sim N(0,1)
. Then by Lemma
7.23
(a), there exists
K_{0}>0
, depending only on
\tau_{1}
and
r
, such that
\psi_{y}\in\mathrm{PL}_{1}(r,K_{0}L_{y})
with
L_{y}:=L(1\vee\lvert y\rvert^{r-1})
. For fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
y_{1},\dotsc,y_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
, define
\breve{L}\equiv\breve{L}(y_{1},\dotsc,y_{n}):=(L_{y_{1}},\dotsc,L_{y_{n}})
. Let
r^{\prime}:=r/(r-1)\in(1,2]
be the Hölder conjugate of
r
,
so that
1/r+1/r^{\prime}=1
, and note that since
\|{\cdot}\|_{p^{\prime}}\leq\|{\cdot}\|_{p}
for
1\leq p\leq p^{\prime}\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\|\breve{L}\|_{\infty}}{n^{1/r^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{\|\breve{L}\|_{2}}{n^{1/r^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{\|\breve{L}\|_{r^{\prime}}}{n^{1/r^{\prime}}}=\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}}=\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\lvert L_{y_{i}}\rvert^{r^{\prime}}\biggr)^{1/r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq L\biggl(1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\lvert y_{i}\rvert^{r}\biggr)^{1/r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=L(1+\|y\|_{n,r}^{r})^{1/r^{\prime}}.

(148)

By Lemma
7.12
, there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that if
Z_{1},\dotsc,Z_{n}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}N(0,1)
, then

\displaystyle P(n,t,y_{1},\dotsc,y_{n})

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\bar{\psi}_{y_{i}}(Z_{i})\biggr|\geq t\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\biggl(1-\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{nt}{(Cr)^{r}K_{0}\|\breve{L}\|_{2}}\biggr)^{2},\biggl(\frac{nt}{(Cr)^{r}K_{0}\|\breve{L}\|_{\infty}}\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\biggl(1-\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{n^{1/r}t}{(Cr)^{r}K_{0}\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}}}\biggr)^{2},\biggl(\frac{n^{1/r}t}{(Cr)^{r}K_{0}\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}}}\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle=:E_{r}(n,t,K_{0}\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}})\equiv E_{r}\bigl(n,t,K_{0}\|\breve{L}(y_{1},\dotsc,y_{n})\|_{n,r^{\prime}}\bigr)

(149)

for every
t\geq 0
. Returning to (
147
), we see that

\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\psi_{\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi_{\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\bar{\psi}_{\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1})

for all
1\leq i\leq n
, where the final equality follows from Lemma
7.7
and the fact that
\tilde{Z}^{1}\equiv\tilde{Z}^{1}(n)
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{0}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0})
. We deduce from this and (
149
) that

\mathbb{P}(\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\,|\,\mathscr{S}_{0})=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\psi}_{\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1})\biggr|\geq\varepsilon\Bigm|\mathscr{S}_{0}\biggr)=P(n,\varepsilon,\gamma_{1},\dotsc,\gamma_{n})\leq E_{r}\bigl(n,\varepsilon,K_{0}\breve{L}_{0}(n)\bigr)

(150)

for every
n
and
\varepsilon>0
, where the second equality is again obtained using Lemma
7.7
, and

\breve{L}_{0}(n):=\|\breve{L}(\gamma_{1},\dotsc,\gamma_{n})\|_{n,r^{\prime}}\leq L(1+\|\gamma\|_{n,r}^{r})^{1/r^{\prime}}=O_{c}(1)
by (
148
) and
(A1)
. Thus, by Proposition
1.2
, there exists
\bar{L}_{0}\in(0,\infty)
such that for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the events
A_{0}(n):=\{\breve{L}_{0}(n)\leq\bar{L}_{0}\}\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)
satisfy
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigl(A_{0}(n)^{c}\bigr)<\infty
. Moreover, for each
n
and
\varepsilon>0
, it follows from (
150
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\{\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\}\cap A_{0}(n)\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\mathbb{P}(\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\,|\,\mathscr{S}_{0})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{0}(n)}

\displaystyle\leq P(n,\varepsilon,\gamma_{1},\dotsc,\gamma_{n})\mathbbm{1}_{A_{0}(n)}

\displaystyle\leq E_{r}\bigl(n,\varepsilon,K_{0}\breve{L}_{0}(n)\bigr)\mathbbm{1}_{A_{0}(n)}\leq E_{r}(n,\varepsilon,K_{0}\bar{L}_{0}),

where we have used the fact that
A_{0}(n)\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)
to obtain the first equality above. Recalling the expression for
E_{r}(n,\varepsilon,K_{0}\bar{L}_{0})
in (
149
), we see that
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}E_{r}(n,\varepsilon,K_{0}\bar{L}_{0})<\infty
, and hence conclude that for every
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}(\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\{\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\}\cap A_{0}(n)\bigr)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(A_{0}(n)^{c}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\mathbb{P}\bigl(\{\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\}\cap A_{0}(n)\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)\bigr\}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(A_{0}(n)^{c}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,E_{r}(n,\varepsilon,K_{0}\bar{L}_{0})+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(A_{0}(n)^{c}\bigr)<\infty,

(151)

which together with Proposition
1.2
implies (
147
). Together with (
146
), this shows that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
, as claimed.

Next, we bound
\lvert T_{n2}\rvert
for each
n
. Letting
L>0
be such that
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(r,L)
, we can apply Lemma
7.24
to see that

\displaystyle\lvert T_{n2}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\bigl|\psi(h_{i}^{1,0},\gamma_{i})-\psi(h_{i}^{1,0}-\Delta_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\frac{r}{2}-1}L\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}\bigl(1+\|h^{1,0}\|_{n,r}^{r-1}+\|h^{1,0}-\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}^{r-1}+2\|\gamma\|_{n,r}^{r-1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{r}L\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}\bigl(1+\|h^{1,0}\|_{n,r}^{r-1}+\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}^{r-1}+\|\gamma\|_{n,r}^{r-1}\bigr),

(152)

where the final bound is obtained using the triangle inequality for
\|{\cdot}\|_{n,r}
and the fact that
(a+b)^{r-1}\leq 2^{r-2}(a^{r-1}+b^{r-1})
for
a,b\geq 0
. Now
\|h^{1,0}\|_{n,r}\overset{d}{=}\|h^{1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
by
\mathcal{H}_{1}(b)
and
\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}=o_{c}(1)
by
\mathcal{H}_{1}(a)
, so
\lvert T_{n2}\rvert=o_{c}(1)\bigl(1+O_{c}(1)+o_{c}(1)\bigr)=o_{c}(1)
. We conclude that
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(h_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\overset{d}{=}T_{n}=T_{n1}+T_{n2}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
, as desired.

\mathcal{H}_{1}(d)
: For each
n
, we have
(\gamma,m^{0},h^{1})\overset{d}{=}(\gamma,m^{0},h^{1,0})
by (
145
) and Lemma
7.6
(c), so for each fixed
\phi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(1)
, it follows that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\,\phi(h_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\overset{d}{=}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\,\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,m_{i}^{0}\bigl\{\phi(h_{i}^{1,0},\gamma_{i})-\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\bigr\}=:T_{n1}+T_{n2}.

(153)

By similar (and slightly simpler) arguments to those in
\mathcal{H}_{1}(c)
, we will prove that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot\phi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
and
T_{n2}\overset{c}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

For
T_{n1}
, define
\Phi\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\Phi(y):=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\phi(\tau_{1}Z,y)\bigr)
with
Z\sim N(0,1)
. For each
n
, recalling once again that
\tilde{Z}^{1}\equiv\tilde{Z}^{1}(n)\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{0}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0})
, we deduce from Lemma
7.7
that
\mathbb{E}\bigl(\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\Phi(\gamma_{i})
almost surely, for every
1\leq i\leq n
. Now since
\Phi
is Lipschitz by Lemma
7.23
(b), it follows from
(A3)
that if
\bar{G}_{1}\sim N(0,\tau_{1}^{2})
is independent of
\bar{\gamma}\sim\pi
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl(\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\,\Phi(\gamma_{i})

\displaystyle\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\Phi(\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\phi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\!\bigm|\!\bar{\gamma}\bigr\}\bigl)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot\phi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr).

(154)

To complete the proof that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot\phi(\bar{G}_{1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
, we must therefore show that

T_{n1}^{\prime}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\bigl\{\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)\bigr\}\overset{c}{\to}0.

(155)

To this end, let
L>0
be such that
\phi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(1,L)
on
\mathbb{R}
. For
u,y\in\mathbb{R}
, define
\phi_{u,y}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\phi_{u,y}(z):=u\bigl\{\phi(\tau_{1}z,y)-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\phi(\tau_{1}Z,y)\bigr)\bigr\}
, where
Z\sim N(0,1)
, so that
\phi_{u,y}\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(1,L\tau_{1}\lvert u\rvert)
.
Since
\tilde{Z}^{1}\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
, it follows from (
201
) in Remark
7.13
that for every
v\equiv(v_{1},\dotsc,v_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{P}(n,t,v):=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{v_{i},y_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1})\biggr|\geq t\biggr)\leq\exp\biggl\{1-\biggl(\frac{nt}{CL\tau_{1}\|v\|_{2}}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\biggl\{1-\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}t}{CL\tau_{1}\|v\|_{n}}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=:\tilde{E}_{r}(n,t,L\tau_{1}\|v\|_{n}),

(156)

where
C>0
is a suitable universal constant. Recalling once again that
\tilde{Z}^{1}
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{0}=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0})
, we deduce using Lemma
7.7
that

m_{i}^{0}\bigl\{\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\phi(\tilde{h}_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)\bigr\}=\phi_{m_{i}^{0},\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1})
for all
1\leq i\leq n
. Thus, for each
n
and
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\,|\,\mathscr{S}_{0})=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{m_{i}^{0},\gamma_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{1})\biggr|\geq\varepsilon\Bigm|\mathscr{S}_{0}\biggr)=\tilde{P}(n,\varepsilon,m^{0})\leq\tilde{E}_{r}(n,\varepsilon,L\tau_{1}\|m^{0}\|_{n}).

(157)

Since
\|m^{0}\|_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\tau_{1}
by
(A2)
, Proposition
1.2
ensures that the events
\tilde{A}_{0}(n):=\{\|m^{0}\|_{n}\leq\tau_{1}+1\}\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)
satisfy
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\tilde{A}_{0}(n)^{c}\bigr)<\infty
. Moreover, for each
n
and
\varepsilon>0
, it follows from (
156
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\{\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\}\cap\tilde{A}_{0}(n)\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{0}\bigr)=\mathbb{P}(\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\,|\,\mathscr{S}_{0})\mathbbm{1}_{\tilde{A}_{0}(n)}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{P}(n,\varepsilon,m^{0})\mathbbm{1}_{\tilde{A}_{0}(n)}

(158)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{E}_{r}(n,\varepsilon,L\tau_{1}C_{0})\mathbbm{1}_{\tilde{A}_{0}(n)}\leq\tilde{E}_{r}(n,\varepsilon,L\tau_{1}C_{0}),

where we have used the fact that
\tilde{A}_{0}(n)\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(n)
to obtain the first equality above. Recalling the expression for
\tilde{E}_{r}(n,\varepsilon,C_{0})
in (
156
), we see that
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\tilde{E}_{r}(n,\varepsilon,L\tau_{1}C_{0})<\infty
. Thus, for every
\varepsilon>0
, we conclude as in (
151
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}(\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\{\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\}\cap\tilde{A}_{0}(n)\bigr)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\tilde{A}_{0}(n)^{c}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\tilde{E}_{r}(n,\varepsilon,L\tau_{1}C_{0})+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\tilde{A}_{0}(n)^{c}\bigr)<\infty,

(159)

which implies (
155
) in view of Proposition
1.2
, and hence that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\tilde{\tau}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl(\phi(\bar{G}_{1})\bigr)
in (
153
).

As for
T_{n2}
in (
153
), let
L>0
be as above, so that
\phi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(1,L)
. For each
n
, recalling from (
145
) that
h^{1,0}=\tilde{h}^{1}+\Delta^{1}
, we now apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to see that

\displaystyle\lvert T_{n2}\rvert\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\lvert m_{i}^{0}\rvert\,\bigl|\phi(h_{i}^{1,0},\gamma_{i})-\phi(h_{i}^{1,0}-\Delta_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i})\bigr|\leq\frac{L}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\lvert m_{i}^{0}\rvert\,\lvert\Delta_{i}^{1}\rvert\leq L\|m^{0}\|_{n}\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n}.

Since
\|m^{0}\|_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\tau_{1}
by
(A2)
and
\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n}\leq\|\Delta^{1}\|_{n,r}=o_{c}(1)
by
\mathcal{H}_{1}(a)
, we conclude that
T_{n2}=O_{c}(1)\,o_{c}(1)=o_{c}(1)
. This completes the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(d)
.

Turning to the inductive step, we consider a general
k\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose that
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b,c,d)
have already been established. The assertions
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e,\dotsc,j)
and
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(a,b,c,d)
will now be proved, in that order. Note that
\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(2)\subseteq\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(r)
since
r\geq 2
.

\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
: In the case
j=\ell=1
, we have
\bigl|\|m^{0}\|_{n}-\tau_{1}\bigr|\overset{c}{\to}0
by
(A2)
, so
\langle m^{0},m^{0}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\tau_{1}^{2}=\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{1,1}=\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{1}^{2})
by (
139
). Now fix
j,\ell\in\{2,\dotsc,k+1\}
. Then
\tilde{\psi}_{j\ell}\colon(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},y)\mapsto f_{j-1}(x_{j-1},y)f_{\ell-1}(x_{\ell-1},y)
lies in
\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(2)\subseteq\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(r)
by Lemma
7.22
and the fact that
f_{j-1},f_{\ell-1}
in the AMP recursion (
5
) are Lipschitz by assumption. Thus, by taking
\psi=\tilde{\psi}_{j\ell}
in
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
, we see that

\displaystyle\langle m^{j-1},m^{\ell-1}\rangle_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{j-1}(h_{i}^{j-1},\gamma_{i})\,f_{\ell-1}(h_{i}^{\ell-1},\gamma_{i})

\displaystyle\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{j-1}(\bar{G}_{j-1},\bar{\gamma})\cdot f_{\ell-1}(\bar{G}_{\ell-1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}=\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{j}\bar{G}_{\ell}),

where the final equalities are taken from (
139
). To handle the remaining case where
\{j,\ell\}=\{1,k+1\}
, note that since
f_{k}
is Lipschitz, the map
\phi_{k+1}\colon(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},y)\mapsto f_{k}(x_{k},y)
lies in
\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(1)
. Thus, by taking
\phi=\phi_{k+1}
in
\mathcal{H}_{k}(d)
, we deduce that

\langle m^{0},m^{k}\rangle_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\,f_{k}(h_{i}^{k},\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\cdot f_{k}(\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{1,k+1}=\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{1}\bar{G}_{k+1}),

where the final equalities are again taken from (
139
).

\mathcal{H}_{k}(f)
: This proof is very similar to that of
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
. First fix
1\leq j,\ell\leq k
. By Lemma
7.22
, the function
(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},y)\mapsto x_{j}f_{\ell}(x_{\ell},y)
lies in
\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(2)\subseteq\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(r)
, so by applying
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
again, we deduce that

\langle h^{j},m^{\ell}\rangle_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{j}\,f_{\ell}(h_{i}^{\ell},\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bar{G}_{j}f_{\ell}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{\ell}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{\ell},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)\mathbb{E}(\bar{G}_{j}\bar{G}_{\ell})=\bar{b}_{\ell}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell},

where the final equalities are taken from (
141
). For the second part of
\mathcal{H}_{k}(f)
, we fix
1\leq j\leq k
and apply
\mathcal{H}_{k}(d)
with the
\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(1)
function
(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},y)\mapsto x_{j}
to see that

\langle h^{j},m^{0}\rangle_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}m_{i}^{0}\,h_{i}^{j}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(F_{0}(\bar{\gamma})\bar{G}_{j}\bigr)=0

by the independence of
\bar{G}_{j}\sim N(0,\tau_{j}^{2})
and
\bar{\gamma}\sim\pi
.

\mathcal{H}_{k}(g)
: In view of Definition
1.1
of complete convergence, it suffices to show that if
(\beta_{n})
is any sequence of random variables with
\beta_{n}\overset{d}{=}\langle f_{k}^{\prime}(h^{k},\gamma)\rangle_{n}
for each
n
, then
\beta_{n}\overset{a.s.}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{k}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
. For any such sequence
(\beta_{n})
, we first seek to construct a random sequence
\bigl((\eta_{n},\theta_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\bigr)
such that
(\eta_{n},\theta_{n})\overset{d}{=}\bigl(h^{k}(n),\gamma(n)\bigr)
for each
n
, and
\bigl(\langle f_{k}^{\prime}(\eta_{n},\theta_{n})\rangle_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\bigr)=(\beta_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N})
almost surely as random sequences. This can be done by applying Lemma
7.10
, where we take
g_{n}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
to be the measurable function
(x,y)\mapsto\langle f_{k}^{\prime}(x,y)\rangle_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{k}^{\prime}(x_{i},y_{i})
for each
n
.

Since
(\eta_{n},\theta_{n})\overset{d}{=}\bigl(h^{k}(n),\gamma(n)\bigr)
for each
n
by construction, it follows from the inductive hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
that
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(\eta_{ni},\theta_{ni})\overset{d}{=}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(h_{i}^{k},\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\varphi(\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
for every
\varphi\in\mathrm{PL}_{2}(r)
,
where
(\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\sim N(0,\tau_{k}^{2})\otimes\pi=:\bar{\mu}^{k}
. Consequently, denoting by
\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{k}:=\nu_{n}(\eta_{n},\theta_{n})=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(\eta_{ni},\theta_{ni})}
the joint empirical distribution of the components of
\eta_{n}
and
\theta_{n}
for each
n
, we deduce using Corollary
7.21
(a) that
d_{r}(\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{k},\bar{\mu}^{k})\overset{a.s.}{\to}0
, and hence that
(\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{k})
converges weakly to
\bar{\mu}^{k}
with probability 1. By
(A5)
,
f_{k}^{\prime}
is bounded, Borel measurable and continuous
\bar{\mu}^{k}
-almost everywhere, so we may now apply Lemma
7.14
to conclude that
\beta_{n}=\langle f_{k}^{\prime}(\eta_{n},\theta_{n})\rangle_{n}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{k}^{\prime}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{n}^{k}\to\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f_{k}^{\prime}\,d\bar{\mu}^{k}=\mathbb{E}\bigl(f_{k}^{\prime}(\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
almost surely. This completes the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{k}(g)
.

\mathcal{H}_{k}(h)
: For
1\leq\ell\leq k
, it follows from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e,f,g)
that

v_{j}^{k,\ell}/n=\langle h^{j},m^{\ell}\rangle_{n}-b_{\ell}\,\langle m^{j-1},m^{\ell-1}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{b}_{\ell}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}-\bar{b}_{\ell}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}=0

for all
1\leq j\leq k
. For
\ell=0
, we have
m^{-1}=0
by definition, so
v_{j}^{k,0}/n=\langle h^{j},m^{0}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}0
for all
1\leq j\leq k
by the second part of
\mathcal{H}_{k}(f)
.

\mathcal{H}_{k}(i)
: Recall from (
126
) that
\alpha^{k}=(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)^{+}(M_{k}^{\top}m^{k}/n)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
. It follows from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
that
(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)_{j\ell}=\langle m^{j-1},m^{\ell-1}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}
and
(M_{k}^{\top}m^{k}/n)_{j}=\langle m^{j-1},m^{k}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,k+1}
for all
1\leq j,\ell\leq k
. In the notation of Section
6.4
, this means that
M_{k}^{\top}m^{k}/n\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
. Under
(A4)
, Lemma
2.2
ensures that
\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}
is positive definite and hence invertible, we now apply the continuous mapping theorem for complete convergence (Lemma
7.2
) to deduce that

\alpha^{k}=(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)^{+}(M_{k}^{\top}m^{k}/n)\overset{c}{\to}\bigl(\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\bigr)^{-1}\,\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k],k+1}=\bar{\alpha}^{k},

as defined in (
136
).

\mathcal{H}_{k}(j)
: Recalling (
126
) as well as the definitions at the start of Section
6.2
, we can write

\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}^{2}=\|P_{k}^{\perp}m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}=\|m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}-\|P_{k}m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}=\|m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}-(\alpha^{k})^{\top}(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)\,\alpha^{k}.

Now
\|m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k+1,k+1}=\tau_{k+1}^{2}
and
M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
, and
\alpha^{k}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\alpha}^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(i)
, so

\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}^{2}=\|m^{k}\|_{n}^{2}-(\alpha^{k})^{\top}(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)\,\alpha^{k}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{k+1,k+1}-(\bar{\alpha}^{k})^{\top}\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\,\bar{\alpha}^{k}=\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}_{k+1},

as defined in (
137
).

\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(a)
: Denote by
R_{n1},\dotsc,R_{n5}
the individual summands (in the order in which they appear) in the definition (
144
) of
\Delta^{k+1}\equiv\Delta^{k+1}(n)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. To establish that
\|\Delta^{k+1}\|_{n,r}\overset{c}{\to}0
, it suffices to show that
\|R_{ns}\|_{n,r}\overset{c}{\to}0
for
s=1,\dotsc,5
. Observe first that since
\alpha^{k}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\alpha}^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(i)
and
\|h^{\ell}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
for all
1\leq\ell\leq k
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b)
, we have

\|R_{n1}\|_{n,r}\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\,\lvert\alpha_{\ell}^{k}-\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\rvert\,\|h^{\ell}\|_{n,r}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}o_{c}(1)\,O_{c}(1)=o_{c}(1)
.

As for
R_{n2}
, we know from
\mathcal{H}_{k}(e)
that
(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)_{j\ell}=\langle m^{j-1},m^{\ell-1}\rangle_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}_{j,\ell}
for all
1\leq j,\ell\leq k
, so
M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n\overset{c}{\to}\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, which is positive definite by Lemma
2.2
. We can now apply the continuous mapping theorem for complete convergence (Lemma
7.2
) to deduce that
(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k}/n)^{+}\overset{c}{\to}(\bar{\mathrm{T}}^{[k]})^{-1}
; see
\mathcal{H}_{k}(i)
above for a similar argument. By
\mathcal{H}_{k}(h)
, we have
v^{k,\ell}/n\overset{c}{\to}0\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
for all
0\leq\ell\leq k
, so

\tilde{w}^{k}\equiv\tilde{w}^{k}(n):=(M_{k}^{\top}M_{k})^{+}\biggl(v^{k,k}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\,v^{k,\ell-1}\biggr)\overset{c}{\to}0,

formally by Slutsky’s lemma for complete convergence (Lemma
7.2
).
Since
\|m^{\ell-1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
for
1\leq\ell\leq k
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b)
, we have
\|R_{n2}\|_{n,r}=\|M_{k}\tilde{w}^{k}\|_{n,r}\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\,\lvert\tilde{w}_{\ell}^{k}\rvert\,\|m^{\ell-1}\|_{n,r}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}o_{c}(1)\,O_{c}(1)=o_{c}(1)
.

Turning to
R_{n3}
and introducing
\xi_{1},\dotsc,\xi_{k}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}N(0,1)
, we see from Lemma
6.18
that
\|P_{k}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\|_{n,r}
is stochastically dominated by
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\,\lvert\xi_{i}\rvert/n^{1/r}
for each
n>k
. By Example
1
(a),
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\,\lvert\xi_{i}\rvert/n^{1/r}\leq k\max_{1\leq i\leq k}\,\lvert\xi_{i}\rvert/n^{1/r}=o_{c}(1)
, so
\|P_{k}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\|_{n,r}\overset{c}{\to}0
. Since
\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\in(0,\infty)
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(j)
, we deduce that
\|R_{n3}\|_{n,r}=\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}\|P_{k}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=o_{c}(1)
.

For the remaining summands
R_{n4}
and
R_{n5}
, the arguments are similar to those in the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(a)
. Recall that
(\tilde{Z}^{k+1},\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1})\equiv\bigl(\tilde{Z}^{k+1}(n),\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}(n)\bigr)\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})\otimes N(0,1/n)
. Introducing
\zeta\sim N(0,1)
, we have
\lvert\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\rvert\overset{d}{=}n^{-1/2}\,\lvert\zeta\rvert\overset{c}{\to}0
by Example
1
(a), and
\|\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\lvert\tilde{Z}_{i}^{k+1}\rvert^{r})^{1/r}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}(\lvert\zeta\rvert^{r})^{1/r}\in(0,\infty)
by Lemma
7.12
and Proposition
1.2
. By
\mathcal{H}_{k}(j)
, we have
\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}-\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}=o_{c}(1)
. Moreover,
\alpha^{k}=\bar{\alpha}^{k}+o_{c}(1)=O_{c}(1)
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(i)
and
\|m^{\ell}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
for
0\leq\ell\leq k
by
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b)
, so it follows from (
126
) that

\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n,r}=\|(I-P_{k})\,m^{k}\|_{n,r}\leq\|m^{k}\|_{n,r}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\,\lvert\alpha_{\ell}^{k}\rvert\,\|m^{\ell-1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}O_{c}(1)\,O_{c}(1)=O_{c}(1).

Putting everything together, we see that

\|R_{n4}\|_{n,r}+\|R_{n5}\|_{n,r}=\bigl|\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n}-\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\bigr|\,\|\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\|_{n,r}+\lvert\tilde{\zeta}^{k+1}\rvert\,\|\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{m}^{\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\scriptstyle k$}}\|_{n,r}=o_{c}(1)\,O_{c}(1)+o_{c}(1)\,O_{c}(1)=o_{c}(1).

We have now shown that
\|R_{ns}\|_{n,r}\overset{c}{\to}0
for
s=1,\dotsc,5
, so
\|\Delta^{k+1}\|_{n,r}\leq\sum_{s=1}^{5}\|R_{ns}\|_{n,r}\overset{c}{\to}0
.

\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(b)
: By the inductive hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b)
, we have
\|h^{j}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
for all
1\leq j\leq k
and
\|m^{j}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
for all
0\leq j\leq k
. Now let
j=k+1
. For each integer
n>k
, recall from (
129
) in Proposition
6.11
and (
143
) that

h^{k+1}(n)\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{k}}}h^{k+1,k}(n)=\tilde{h}^{k+1}(n)+\Delta^{k+1}(n)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,h^{\ell}(n)+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\tilde{Z}^{k+1}(n)+\Delta^{k+1}(n),

(160)

where the deterministic
\bar{\alpha}^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
is taken from (
136
) and
\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\equiv\tilde{Z}^{k+1}(n)\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{k}(n)=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0},h^{1},\dotsc,h^{k})
. Then
\|\Delta^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=o_{c}(1)
by
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(a)
and
\|\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)
, as in the last part of the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(a)
above. It follows from this and
\mathcal{H}_{k}(b)
that

\|h^{k+1}\|_{n,r}\overset{d}{=}\|h^{k+1,k}\|_{n,r}\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\,\lvert\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\rvert\,\|h^{\ell}\|_{n,r}+\|\Delta^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=O_{c}(1)+o_{c}(1)=O_{c}(1).

In addition, letting
L^{\prime}>0
be such that
f_{k+1}
is
L^{\prime}
-Lipschitz, we can argue as in the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(b)
to deduce that

\|m^{k+1}\|_{n,r}=\|f_{k+1}(h^{k+1},\gamma)\|_{n,r}\leq\lvert f_{k+1}(0,0)\rvert+L^{\prime}(\|h^{k+1}\|_{n,r}+\|\gamma\|_{n,r})=O_{c}(1).

\mathcal{H}_{k+1}(c)
: We again make use of the distributional equality (
160
), which together with Lemma
7.6
(c) implies that
(\gamma,h^{1},\dotsc,h^{k},h^{k+1})\mathrel{\overset{d}{=}\!|_{\mathscr{S}_{k}}}(\gamma,h^{1},\dotsc,h^{k},h^{k+1,k})
for each integer
n>k
. Thus, for any fixed
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{k+2}(r)
, it follows that
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},h_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})\overset{d}{=}n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},h_{i}^{k+1,k},\gamma_{i})=:T_{n}
for each such
n
, so it suffices to show that
T_{n}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{G}_{k+1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
. We decompose
T_{n}
as

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\{\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},h_{i}^{k+1,k},\gamma_{i})-\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})\}=:T_{n1}+T_{n2}

(161)

for each
n>k
, and seek to establish that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{G}_{k+1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
and
T_{n2}\overset{c}{\to}0

by imitating and extending the analogous arguments in the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(c)
.

For
T_{n1}
, define
\Psi\colon\mathbb{R}^{k+1}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\Psi(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},y):=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\psi\bigl(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,x_{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}Z,y\bigr)\bigr\}
with
Z\sim N(0,1)
.
For each integer
n>k
, since
\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\equiv\tilde{Z}^{k+1}(n)\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{k}(n)=\sigma(\gamma,m^{0},h^{1},\dotsc,h^{k})
, we deduce from (
160
) and Lemma
7.7
that

\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{k}\bigr)=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\psi\bigl(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\textstyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,h_{i}^{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\tilde{Z}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i}\bigr)\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{k}\bigr\}=\Psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\gamma_{i})

almost surely, for every
1\leq i\leq n
. Now since
\Psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{k+1}(r)
by Lemma
7.23
(b), it follows from the inductive hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{k}(c)
that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{k}\bigr)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\gamma_{i})\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\Psi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr),

(162)

where the equality above holds almost surely for each
n>k
. Taking
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\zeta}_{k+1}\sim N(0,1)
to be independent of
\bar{G}_{[k]}:=(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k})
and
\bar{\gamma}
, we apply Lemma
7.7
again to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\Psi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\psi\bigl(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\textstyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,\bar{G}_{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\zeta}_{k+1},\bar{\gamma}\bigr)\!\bigm|\!\bar{G}_{[k]},\bar{\gamma}\bigr\}\bigl)

(163)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\psi\bigl(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\textstyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,\bar{G}_{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\zeta}_{k+1},\bar{\gamma}\bigr)\bigr\}=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{G}_{k+1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr),

where the final equality follows from the definition of
\bar{G}_{k+1}
in (
138
). To complete the proof that
T_{n1}\overset{c}{\to}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(\bar{G}_{1},\dotsc,\bar{G}_{k},\bar{G}_{k+1},\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
, we must therefore show that

T_{n1}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\bigl\{\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{k}\bigr)\bigr\}\overset{c}{\to}0.

(164)

To this end, let
L>0
be such that
\psi\in\mathrm{PL}_{k+2}(r,L)
, and for each
v\equiv(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},y)\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
, define
\psi_{v},\bar{\psi}_{v}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\psi_{v}(z):=\psi\bigl(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k},\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bar{\alpha}_{\ell}^{k}\,x_{\ell}+\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}z,y\bigr)
and
\bar{\psi}_{v}(z):=\psi_{v}(z)-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi_{v}(Z)\bigr)
, where
Z\sim N(0,1)
. Then by Lemma
7.23
(a), there exists
K_{k}>0
, depending only on the deterministic
\bar{\alpha}^{k}\equiv(\bar{\alpha}_{1}^{k},\dotsc,\bar{\alpha}_{k}^{k})
,
\overset{\raisebox{-0.7pt}{\scalebox{0.7}[0.35]{$\perp$}}}{\tau}_{k+1}
and
r
, such that
\psi_{v}\in\mathrm{PL}_{1}(r,K_{k}L_{\|v\|})
with
L_{\|v\|}=L(1\vee\|v\|^{r-1})
. For a fixed integer
n>k
and
v^{(1)},\dotsc,v^{(n)}\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
, define
\breve{L}\equiv\breve{L}\bigl(v^{(1)},\dotsc,v^{(n)}\bigr):=\bigl(L_{\|v^{(1)}\|},\dotsc,L_{\|v^{(n)}\|}\bigr)
. Let
r^{\prime}=r/(r-1)\in(1,2]
be as in the proof of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(c)
,
so that
1/r+1/r^{\prime}=1
, and note that since
\|{\cdot}\|_{p^{\prime}}\leq\|{\cdot}\|_{p}
for
1\leq p\leq p^{\prime}\leq\infty
, we have

\frac{\|\breve{L}\|_{\infty}}{n^{1/r^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{\|\breve{L}\|_{2}}{n^{1/r^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{\|\breve{L}\|_{r^{\prime}}}{n^{1/r^{\prime}}}=\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}}=\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\bigl(L_{\|v^{(i)}\|}\bigr)^{r^{\prime}}\biggr)^{1/r^{\prime}}\leq L\biggl(1+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|v^{(i)}\|^{r}\biggr)^{1/r^{\prime}}.

(165)

By Lemma
7.12
, it follows as in (
149
) that there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that if
Z_{1},\dotsc,Z_{n}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}N(0,1)
, then

\displaystyle P\bigl(n,t,v^{(1)},\dotsc,v^{(n)}\bigr)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\bar{\psi}_{v^{(i)}}(Z_{i})\biggr|\geq t\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\biggl(1-\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{nt}{(Cr)^{r}K_{k}\|\breve{L}\|_{2}}\biggr)^{2},\biggl(\frac{nt}{(Cr)^{r}K_{k}\|\breve{L}\|_{\infty}}\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\biggl(1-\min\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{n^{1/r}t}{(Cr)^{r}K_{k}\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}}}\biggr)^{2},\biggl(\frac{n^{1/r}t}{(Cr)^{r}K_{k}\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}}}\biggr)^{2/r}\biggr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle=E_{r}(n,t,K_{k}\|\breve{L}\|_{n,r^{\prime}})\equiv E_{r}\Bigl(n,t,K_{k}\bigl\|\breve{L}\bigl(v^{(1)},\dotsc,v^{(n)}\bigr)\bigr\|_{n,r^{\prime}}\Bigr)

(166)

for every
t\geq 0
. Next, define the
\mathscr{S}_{k}
-measurable vectors
\upsilon_{k}^{(i)}:=(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\gamma_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
Returning to (
164
) and recalling (
160
), we see that

\displaystyle\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi(h_{i}^{1},\dotsc,h_{i}^{k},\tilde{h}_{i}^{k+1},\gamma_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{k}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\upsilon_{k}^{(i)}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{k+1})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(\psi_{\upsilon_{k}^{(i)}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{k+1})\!\bigm|\!\mathscr{S}_{k}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bar{\psi}_{\upsilon_{k}^{(i)}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{k+1})

for all
1\leq i\leq n
, where the final equality follows from Lemma
7.7
and the fact that
\tilde{Z}^{k+1}\equiv\tilde{Z}^{k+1}(n)
is independent of
\mathscr{S}_{k}\equiv\mathscr{S}_{k}(n)
. We deduce from this and (
166
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\lvert T_{n1}^{\prime}\rvert>\varepsilon\,|\,\mathscr{S}_{k})=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{\psi}_{\upsilon_{k}^{(i)}}(\tilde{Z}_{i}^{k+1})\biggr|\geq\varepsilon\Bigm|\mathscr{S}_{k}\biggr)

\displaystyle=P\bigl(n,\varepsilon,\upsilon_{k}^{(1)},\dotsc,\upsilon_{k}^{(n)}\bigr)
